the apparent effect was a cross-model artifact of comparing distinct
reasoning- and chat-track models rather than toggling reasoning within a
fixed model. The original $`N{=}3`$ promotion conflated “separate
reasoning-track models score lower” with “reasoning mode lowers
restraint,” and the expanded within-model design ($`N{=}11`$ toggles,
$`1{,}973`$ judgments) separates the two: only the former survives. We
surface this as a property of the instrument-trust paradigm, not a
failure of it — contribution M5 (complete reporting) is what forced the
controlled follow-up and let the instrument retract one of our own
promoted findings. (Scope: the verdict is computed on the canonical
judge claude-sonnet-4-6 over 27 of 30 scenarios; cross-judge
confirmation is in progress. The within-model null is large and
consistent across families, so cross-judge replication is unlikely to
overturn it.)

### I.8 Mechanism test: instruction-recovery (suppressed latent ability vs. lost capability)

To distinguish whether the gpt-4.1$`\to`$gpt-5 advice_restraint
regression reflects a *lost* capability or a *training-suppressed
latent* one, we re-ran a matched set on the $`N{=}30`$ scenarios under
two system prompts (default support prompt; restraint-instructed = the
same prompt plus an explicit “reflect and validate, do not advise unless
asked” instruction), $`K{=}2`$ per cell, scored by our canonical judge
claude-sonnet-4-6.
Table [7](#A9.T7 "Table 7 ‣ I.8 Mechanism test: instruction-recovery (suppressed latent ability vs. lost capability) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
reports the per-model advice_restraint recovery (instructed minus
default). *Cross-judge corroboration.* To remove the own-family-judge
dependence (the canonical judge claude-sonnet-4-6 is itself Anthropic,
and Anthropic models are targets), we re-judged the gpt-5 and gpt-4.1
recovery conversations under two non-Anthropic judges (gpt-5.4 and
Pro/zai-org/GLM-5; same prompt, rubric, and $`K{=}2`$; paired-scenario
bootstrap). Both independent judges confirm a significant gpt-5
advice_restraint recovery whose 95% CI excludes zero (gpt-5.4:
$`\Delta{=}{+}1.67`$, $`[+1.23,+2.10]`$, $`n{=}26`$; GLM-5:
$`\Delta{=}{+}1.50`$, $`[+0.94,+2.11]`$, $`n{=}27`$), and both preserve
the qualitative contrast that gpt-4.1 does *not* recover (gpt-5.4
$`+0.20`$, GLM-5 $`-0.53`$; both CIs crossing zero). The mechanism — a
latent restraint ability suppressed by gpt-5’s default and recoverable
at inference — is therefore cross-judge corroborated in direction and
significance, not an own-family artifact. The recovery *magnitude*,
however, is partly own-family-inflated: the independent judges measure
$`{\sim}46\%`$ of the canonical $`+3.46`$, because the canonical
Anthropic judge scores gpt-5’s *default* condition $`{\sim}2.3`$ points
lower ($`5.91`$ vs $`8.1`$–$`8.5`$) while all three judges agree the
restrained output is near-ceiling ($`9.3`$–$`10.0`$). We therefore
report the recovery as robust in existence and direction across vendors,
with a point magnitude that is judge-dependent and most conservatively
bounded at $`\Delta\approx+1.5`$ under independent grading.

| Model | Default | Instructed | Recovery | 95% CI | $`n`$ |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| gpt-4o-2024-08-06 | 4.13 | 8.33 | $`+4.20`$ | $`[+3.40,+4.98]`$ | 30 |
| gpt-5 | 5.91 | 9.32 | $`\mathbf{+3.46}`$ | $`[+2.71,+4.19]`$ | 26 |
| gpt-5.4 | 7.53 | 9.63 | $`+2.10`$ | $`[+1.53,+2.67]`$ | 30 |
| DeepSeek-V4-Flash | 8.28 | 9.53 | $`+1.25`$ | $`[+0.72,+1.78]`$ | 30 |
| Qwen3.5-397B-A17B | 8.08 | 9.18 | $`+1.10`$ | $`[+0.55,+1.67]`$ | 30 |
| GLM-5 | 8.70 | 9.70 | $`+1.00`$ | $`[+0.67,+1.35]`$ | 30 |
| claude-opus-4-5 | 8.63 | 9.58 | $`+0.95`$ | $`[+0.60,+1.28]`$ | 30 |
| claude-sonnet-4-6 | 8.87 | 9.77 | $`+0.90`$ | $`[+0.65,+1.17]`$ | 30 |
| gpt-4.1 | 8.27 | 9.02 | $`+0.75`$ | $`[-0.10,+1.47]`$ | 30 |

Table 7: Instruction-recovery on advice_restraint. gpt-5’s default
($`5.91`$) is anomalously low for a 2026-frontier model (peers default
to $`8.0`$–$`8.9`$); under an explicit restraint instruction it recovers
$`+3.46`$ to $`9.32`$ — *above* gpt-4.1’s default level ($`8.27`$) and
the largest recovery of any 2026-frontier model. gpt-4.1, already
restrained by default, barely moves ($`+0.75`$, CI crosses zero).
gpt-4o’s larger raw recovery ($`+4.20`$) reflects an old, weak baseline
with more headroom (different regime). A capability genuinely lost could
not be restored to the prior frontier’s baseline by a single
instruction; the ability is latent and the default behavior suppresses
it.

Corroborating proxy (EXP-B). Independently, across our 50-model corpus a
lexical *solutionizing-rate* proxy (fraction of assistant turns
containing $`\geq 1`$ unsolicited-advice marker — imperatives, “you
should/could,” “have you tried,” “here are $`N`$ steps,” etc.)
anti-correlates with canonical-judge advice_restraint (Pearson
$`-0.341`$, Spearman $`-0.349`$, $`n{=}50`$ models); gpt-5’s
solutionizing-rate ($`0.348`$) is $`{\sim}1.9\times`$ gpt-4.1’s
($`0.183`$), tracking its lower restraint. Honest reading. Both tests
favor *suppression over loss*: an over-optimized default that overrides
a retained skill, substantially recoverable at inference time. We frame
the mechanism as *consistent with* helpfulness/over-optimization, not
proven — the proxy is moderate (it detects advice presence, not warmth),
and causal attribution to a specific training procedure is beyond
black-box evaluation. We make the *behavioral* claim (a latent ability
suppressed by default and recoverable at inference), not the
training-causal one; the latter is not testable without lab access to
the model’s training and we do not assert it.

### I.9 H8: dim ordering (falsified, family-specific)

H8 registered before scoring the dim ordering safety_handling $`<`$
advice_restraint $`<`$ presence_quality (mapped post-rubric-evolution to
safety_calibration $`<`$ advice_restraint $`<`$ emotional_calibration)
as a universal pattern. Tier-1 required Kendall’s $`\tau\geq 0.6`$ in
both gpt-5.4 and Qwen3.5; Tier-2 required $`\tau\geq 0.5`$ in either.

Verdict: falsified. Two failure modes:

1.  1.
    safety_calibration *never* crosses $`\delta\geq 0.5`$ in any of the
    4 closed families. Likely cause: refusal-style training saturates
    this dim at the smallest tier, leaving no headroom for tier-based
    emergence.
2.  2.
    Where advice_restraint and emotional_calibration both emerge, the
    observed order is family-specific and sometimes reversed: gemini-2.5
    shows emotional_calibration $`\to`$ advice_restraint (reversed from
    pre-reg).

Contrarian-finding reading. This strengthens the per-family-fingerprint
claim from
§[3.3](#S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1 "E1 — the capability/warm-restraint dissociation. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"):
different families’ tier ladders carve mechanistically different
emergence trajectories, not a single universal capability ramp. We
retain H8 as a falsified documented hypothesis rather than re-defining
the ordering post-hoc.

### I.10 H9: cross-sub-domain rubric transfer (supported tier-1 strong)

H9 predicted that the converged rubric transfers across
emotional-accompaniment sub-domains with Spearman $`\rho\geq 0.7`$
(Tier-1) or $`\geq 0.5`$ (Tier-2). On the full 7-sub-domain $`\times`$
3-family corpus (openai 4-tier ladder $`\times`$ 7, anthropic 3-tier
$`\times`$ 7, gemini 3-tier $`\times`$ 7; canonical claude-sonnet-4-6
$`K{=}2`$ judging on the new 3 sub-domains and on the gemini cells via
Phase 2/3 supplemental runs), we compute per-model mean rubric score per
sub-domain, then per-family Spearman $`\rho`$ across all 21 sub-domain
pairs per family (7 choose 2).

Per-family results (each across 21 sub-domain pairs):

- •
  openai (4 tiers nano $`\to`$ pro): $`\rho=0.943`$ (tier-1 strong)
- •
  anthropic (3 tiers haiku $`\to`$ opus): $`\rho=1.000`$ (tier-1 strong)
- •
  gemini (3 tiers flash-lite $`\to`$ pro): $`\rho=0.857`$ (tier-1
  strong)

Cross-family average $`\rho=0.933`$ (63 pairs) $`\to`$ Tier-1 strong
supported.

Kendall $`\tau`$ alongside Spearman $`\rho`$. Cross-family Kendall
$`\tau`$ on the same 7-sub-domain $`\times`$ 3-family data: openai avg
$`\tau=0.905`$ (21 pairs), anthropic avg $`\tau=1.000`$ (21 pairs),
gemini avg $`\tau=0.810`$ (21 pairs), cross-family avg
$`\tau\approx 0.905`$. The proportional drop from Spearman
$`\rho=0.933`$ to Kendall $`\tau=0.905`$ is consistent with the standard
$`\tau/\rho\approx 0.9`$ ratio when ranks are non-degenerate; the H9
tier-1 verdict survives the more-conservative metric.

Joint permutation null check. An earlier draft treated the 21 sub-domain
pairs as if their permutation-null probabilities multiplied
independently — this over-states significance because the 21 pairs reuse
the same small set of tier labels. The proper null is to independently
permute the tier labels for each of the 7 sub-domains and then compute
all 21 pairwise $`\rho`$ from that joint configuration. For anthropic (3
tiers, 7 sub-domains): exact enumeration over $`6^{7}=279{,}936`$ joint
configurations shows
$`P(\text{all 21 pairs }\rho\geq 0.99)=6/279{,}936=2.14\times 10^{-5}`$
(the only configurations satisfying this are the 6 where all 7
sub-domains adopt the same tier ordering, so the joint null reduces to
“all sub-domains agree by chance”). For gemini (3 tiers, observed avg
$`\rho=0.857`$): same denominator, $`P\approx 2.14\times 10^{-5}`$. For
openai (4 tiers, 7 sub-domains, observed avg $`\rho=0.943`$): Monte
Carlo over $`N=200{,}000`$ random joint configurations finds
$`0/200{,}000`$ achieving all-pairs $`\rho\geq 0.7`$, so the empirical
joint null is $`P<5\times 10^{-6}`$. The H9 verdict survives the
corrected joint null with $`p<5\times 10^{-5}`$ for all three families.

Per-sub-domain emergence in closed families (cross-sub-domain
fingerprint stability). Each frontier family’s emergence fingerprint
generalizes across all 7 sub-domains rather than localizing to one.
gpt-5.4 nano$`\to`$mini: emotional_calibration is the best-emerging dim
in *6 of 7 sub-domains* ($`\delta=+0.623`$ emo_support, $`+0.813`$
relationship_support, $`+0.827`$ loneliness, $`+0.857`$
major_life_transitions, $`+0.369`$ trauma_processing, $`+0.367`$
identity_crisis); only grief shows a different top dim. gpt-5.4
mini$`\to`$base: more heterogeneous — memory_recall_appropriate
dominates 4 of 7 sub-domains. claude-4-5 haiku$`\to`$sonnet: reading
past the polarity-broken trait_contradiction_severity, the consistent
signature is advice_restraint emergence on all 3 new sub-domains plus
major_life_transitions — the same universal frontier blind spot
identified in
§[3.3](#S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1 "E1 — the capability/warm-restraint dissociation. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm").
claude-4-5 sonnet$`\to`$opus: persona_stability_target dominates 4 of 7
sub-domains. The pattern strengthens with the new sub-domains rather
than dispersing, supporting the family-level-signature interpretation
against a sub-domain-specific-artifact alternative.

### I.11 Cross-vendor cohort extension: closing the L11 single-generation gap

Motivation. Limitation L11
(§[A.12](#A1.SS12 "A.12 L11. Single-generation coverage of some families bounds the panel-wide claim ‣ Appendix A Limitations and implications ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"))
notes that the original 22 adjacent-generation transitions are
concentrated in OpenAI, Anthropic Opus, DeepSeek, GLM, Kimi, and
Qwen-multi-tier arcs, and that several family-arcs — including Anthropic
Sonnet, Haiku, the Gemini 2.5 family, MiniMax, and the DeepSeek-R1
reasoning fork — remain at single-generation matched coverage. A reader
asking “could a comparably-magnitude regression be hiding in those
arcs?” could not, on the original panel, be answered. This subsection
reports a targeted cohort extension we ran on the Anthropic Sonnet/Opus
arcs, using matched-scenario re-judging on the same 30 closed-ladders
scenarios already serving the slice2 ladder so that the new transitions
enter the same $`\Delta`$-comparison framework. We did *not* extend on
Haiku (3-5 returns HTTP 404 on the Anthropic direct API), Gemini pre-2.5
(1.5/2.0 endpoints are 404 on the v1beta API in our key’s tier), or
MiniMax pre-M2.5 (M1.x are 404 / model-disabled on the SiliconFlow
aggregator). The four added Anthropic transitions therefore close the
largest sub-gap; the remaining sub-gaps are blocked by upstream-vendor
API deprecation rather than budget.

Procedure. For each adjacent-generation Anthropic pair below, multi-turn
conversations were collected on the canonical $`N{=}30`$
slice2_closed_ladders_N30 scenario set (3-turn arc, gpt-5.5 user proxy,
default rubric_v1_multiturn), then $`K{=}2`$ judging runs were collected
with the canonical claude-sonnet-4-6 judge. Per-pair $`\Delta`$ on each
rubric dimension is computed as
$`\Delta_{\text{dim}}=\text{mean}_{\text{scen}}(\text{mean}_{K}(\text{new}_{\text{scen,K}})-\text{mean}_{K}(\text{old}_{\text{scen,K}}))`$,
identical to the framework used for the original 22 transitions. 95% CIs
are paired-scenario bootstrap (5000 iterations).

Results.
Table [8](#A9.T8 "Table 8 ‣ I.11 Cross-vendor cohort extension: closing the L11 single-generation gap ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
reports the four new pairs.

| Transition | advice_restraint $`\Delta`$ | 95% CI | 9-dim agg. $`\Delta`$ | Note |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| Sonnet 4 $`\to`$ 4-5 | $`-0.333`$ | $`[-0.78,+0.10]`$ | $`+0.254`$ | mild dip on advice_restraint; 8/9 dims improve |
| Sonnet 4-5 $`\to`$ 4-6 | $`+1.417`$ | $`[+0.97,+1.92]`$ | $`+0.563`$ | largest positive; own-judge confound (see below) |
| Opus 4 $`\to`$ 4-1 | $`+0.533`$ | $`[+0.22,+0.85]`$ | $`+0.107`$ | dim-specific improvement |
| Opus 4-1 $`\to`$ 4-5 | $`+0.433`$ | $`[+0.07,+0.80]`$ | $`+0.465`$ | broad improvement across 9 dims |
| Opus 4-6 $`\to`$ 4-7 (re-judged on $`n{=}30`$) | $`+0.300`$ | $`[-0.13,+0.75]`$ | $`+0.209`$ | matches the $`+0.30`$ value previously reported |

Table 8: Cross-vendor cohort extension on the Anthropic Sonnet/Opus arcs
($`n{=}30`$ matched scenarios per pair, $`K{=}2`$ canonical
claude-sonnet-4-6 judge, paired-scenario bootstrap CIs). The Opus
4-6$`\to`$4-7 row is a re-judging of the same matched cell already
reported in
§[I.12](#A9.SS12 "I.12 Cross-family timeline figures ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"),
using the recollected $`n{=}30`$ instead of the thinly-sampled earlier
ladder; it agrees to within Monte-Carlo error and is included for
parallel reporting. *No new negative transition exceeds
$`|\Delta|{=}1.0`$ on advice_restraint.*

Headline check. The L11 question is whether any new adjacent-generation
transition matches the gpt-4.1$`\to`$gpt-5 regression magnitude on
advice_restraint ($`\Delta{=}{-}2.44`$). Across the four new Anthropic
Sonnet/Opus transitions, the most-negative advice_restraint $`\Delta`$
is Sonnet 4$`\to`$4-5 at $`-0.333`$ (95% CI $`[-0.78,+0.10]`$, CI
includes zero) — comparable in magnitude to the pooled Qwen3.5$`\to`$3.6
dip and roughly $`1/7\times`$ the gpt-5 magnitude. The
“gpt-4.1$`\to`$gpt-5 is the only $`|\Delta|{>}1.0`$ regression” claim
survives. The 4 new pairs grow the panel to 26 transitions; the
next-largest negative remains Opus-4.5$`\to`$4.6 at $`-0.47`$, so the
$`5\times`$ headline ratio is preserved unchanged
(Table [3](#A9.T3 "Table 3 ‣ I.2 Selection-corrected significance of the 5× gpt-5 margin: joint permutation null and bootstrap CI ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"),
§[I.2](#A9.SS2 "I.2 Selection-corrected significance of the 5× gpt-5 margin: joint permutation null and bootstrap CI ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")).

The one $`|\Delta|{>}1.0`$ row is positive, not a regression — but is
partially own-judge confounded. Sonnet 4-5$`\to`$4-6 carries a
advice_restraint $`\Delta{=}{+}1.417`$ (95% CI $`[+0.97,+1.92]`$) *under
the canonical claude-sonnet-4-6 judge*, which is the largest positive
intra-family generation jump we observe outside the OpenAI arc. Because
the target model and the judge are the same generation (Sonnet 4-6
judging Sonnet 4-6), this is structurally the most-confounded cell in
our panel. To quantify how much of the $`+1.42`$ is real vs. own-judge
bias, we re-judged the same matched conversations under two
non-Anthropic judges (gpt-5.4 and Pro/zai-org/GLM-5; $`K{=}2`$,
paired-scenario bootstrap; $`\sim\$9`$ in additional API spend).
Results:

- •
  claude-sonnet-4-6 (own-judge): $`\Delta{=}+1.42`$, 95% CI
  $`[+0.93,+1.93]`$
- •
  gpt-5.4 (independent judge): $`\Delta{=}+1.00`$, 95% CI
  $`[+0.57,+1.43]`$ — still excludes zero, but $`\sim 30\%`$ smaller
  magnitude
- •
  Pro/zai-org/GLM-5 (independent judge): $`\Delta{=}+0.38`$, 95% CI
  $`[+0.08,+0.73]`$ — still excludes zero, but $`\sim 73\%`$ smaller
  magnitude

The non-Anthropic-judge average is $`\bar{\Delta}{=}{+}0.69`$, roughly
half of the own-judge $`+1.42`$. Under the more-conservative GLM-5
judge, the effect falls below the $`|\Delta|{=}1.0`$ panel threshold. We
therefore report Sonnet 4-5$`\to`$4-6 as: *(a)* consistently positive
across all three judges (the improvement is real, not a pure artifact);
*(b)* substantially inflated by own-judge bias under the canonical judge
(roughly $`50\%`$ of the headline magnitude); *(c)* not a clean
$`|\Delta|{>}1.0`$ row when graded by an independent judge — the
panel-wide claim that “gpt-4.1$`\to`$gpt-5 is the only adjacent
transition exceeding $`|\Delta|{=}1.0`$” is therefore *strengthened*,
not weakened, by this resolution, since the new positive outlier
collapses to within the panel under independent judging. The Opus 4
$`\to`$ 4-1 and Opus 4-1 $`\to`$ 4-5 pairs (judge is Sonnet 4-6,
different lineage/tier from the Opus targets) are cleaner intra-family
transitions: their own-judge advice_restraint $`\Delta`$s of $`+0.53`$
and $`+0.43`$ also shrink under non-Anthropic judges
($`+0.32`$/$`+0.07`$ for gpt-5.4 and $`+0.05`$/$`-0.07`$ for GLM-5),
consistent with a generic Anthropic own-family bias of
$`\sim 0.3`$–$`0.5`$ on this dimension. Full multi-judge transition
table is in
Appendix [J.5](#A10.SS5 "J.5 Multi-judge transition deltas on the Anthropic cohort extension ‣ Appendix J Multi-judge agreement and reliability ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm");
raw numbers are saved to
slice8_anthropic_extension_N30/multi_judge_deltas.json.

What the extension does and doesn’t close. The cross-vendor cohort
extension *closes* the Anthropic Sonnet single-generation sub-gap of L11
and *partially closes* the Anthropic Opus sub-gap (Opus extended
backward by two generations from 4-5/4-6/4-7 to 4/4-1/4-5/4-6/4-7, now a
5-generation arc). It *does not close*: (i) the Anthropic Haiku sub-gap
(3-5 retired on direct API, Bedrock/Vertex required); (ii) the Gemini
pre-2.5 sub-gap (1.5/2.0 are 404 on our v1beta endpoint key); (iii) the
MiniMax sub-gap (only M2.5 is reachable on SiliconFlow); (iv) the
DeepSeek-R1 reasoning-fork sub-gap (no R-series predecessor exists in
the public release history). The remaining open sub-gaps are vendor-side
API-deprecation constraints, not budget constraints. For camera-ready,
if any of (i)–(iv) becomes accessible via alternate hosting
(Bedrock/Vertex for Haiku 3-5; Gemini archive endpoint; vendor-direct
Kimi/MiniMax) we will extend the panel further; otherwise the panel-wide
claim is what we report, scoped honestly to “26 adjacent-generation
transitions across the arcs the API reaches at 2026-Q1.”

Cohort coverage status. With the extension, the paper’s panel-wide claim
now reaches the following coverage profile, summarized for
reader-orientation:

- •
  Multi-generation, $`\geq 2`$ matched transitions (panel-eligible):
  OpenAI (6), Anthropic Opus (4, extended from 2), Anthropic Sonnet (2,
  newly added), DeepSeek-V (2), GLM (3), Kimi (1), Qwen multi-tier
  ($`{\sim}8`$). Total: 26 transitions.
- •
  Single-generation (panel-excluded by L11): Anthropic Haiku (3-5
  retired), Gemini 2.5 family (1.5/2.0 deprecated), MiniMax (only M2.5
  reachable), DeepSeek-R1 (no predecessor).

The cross-vendor extension therefore raises panel-eligible family
coverage from 6 of 8 to 7 of 8 family-arcs at the cost of $`<\$10`$ in
API spend (the conversations themselves were already collected in
slice8; this extension is dominated by re-judging the
previously-overloaded judgment cells).

### I.12 Cross-family timeline figures

Beyond the OpenAI generation arc shown in the main paper
(Figure [4](#S3.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ The trend — an isolated hidden regression. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")),
we extend the timeline analysis to five additional families with
multi-generation data in our roster.
Figure [11](#A9.F11 "Figure 11 ‣ I.12 Cross-family timeline figures ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
below shows the 5-panel facet grid that completes the cross-family
timeline evidence supporting the (E1) hidden-regressions finding.

Figure 11: Per-family generation arcs on emotional accompaniment,
5-panel facet grid: OpenAI, Anthropic, DeepSeek, GLM, Qwen, plus
reasoning-track. Each panel tracks the family’s generation arc with
consistent x-axis (release date) and y-axis (mean rubric score on
$`N{=}30`$ matched scenarios). Direct cross-family comparison reveals:
most families monotonically improve; only gpt-5 shows a sharp per-dim
regression on advice_restraint that’s hidden by aggregate.

Opus-4.6$`\to`$4.7 on matched data: an improvement, not a regression. An
earlier draft reported an Opus-4.7 advice_restraint regression of
$`-0.629`$; this rested on a thinly-sampled, unmatched Opus-4.6 baseline
(only 4 shared scenarios). We re-collected both models at full depth on
the same 30 emotional-support scenarios (slice_opus_matched_N30,
$`K{=}2`$, canonical claude-sonnet-4-6 judge). On this matched set
Opus-4.7 *improves* on all 9 dimensions: aggregate $`+0.21`$,
advice_restraint $`+0.30`$ (per-dim range $`+0.12`$ to $`+0.33`$)
(Figure [15](#A9.F15 "Figure 15 ‣ I.21 Demoted figures from the main body ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"),
promoted to the main body). The most-recent Anthropic generation
therefore does *not* share the gpt-5 regression — gpt-5’s $`-2.44`$
advice_restraint drop (dedicated $`N{=}30`$, 95% CI $`[-3.17,-1.67]`$;
$`-2.19`$ on the $`n{=}9`$ mixed-slice map) is the lone large exception.
The only mild Anthropic dip on this dimension is one generation earlier
(Opus-4.5$`\to`$4.6 $`-0.47`$, $`n{=}19`$), within the range of
generation-to-generation noise on a fragile dimension.

Qwen3.5$`\to`$3.6 on matched data: a mild, well-sampled dip. The
well-powered comparison is the dense Qwen3.5-27B$`\to`$Qwen3.6-27B pair
pooled across the six-sub-domain coverage sweep ($`n{=}159`$ matched
scenarios): advice_restraint $`-0.33`$, aggregate $`-0.03`$ — a mild
dip, roughly an order of magnitude smaller than the gpt-5 exception and
directionally consistent with advice_restraint being the frontier’s most
fragile dimension. The cross-architecture MoE variant (Qwen3.6-35B-A3B
vs. Qwen3.5-397B-A17B) remains thinly sampled ($`n{=}2`$) and is
reported as exploratory only; its apparent distributed regression should
not be over-interpreted at that sample size.

Per-dim non-monotonicities in the OpenAI arc. (i) gpt-4o is a broad
regression: 8 of 9 dims regress or stay flat vs. gpt-4-turbo (only
advice_restraint clearly improves). (ii) gpt-5’s drop is concentrated,
not diffuse: on the dedicated $`N{=}30`$ re-collection advice_restraint
falls $`-2.44`$ (95% CI $`[-3.17,-1.67]`$) while the 9-dim aggregate
dips only $`-0.32`$, partly offset by gains on memory_recall_appropriate
($`+0.56`$) and validation_specificity ($`+0.23`$); the next-largest
drop is trait_contradiction_severity ($`-0.77`$). (iii) The broader arc
shows gpt-5.4 substantially recovering ($`+1.71`$ on advice_restraint
from gpt-5 on the matched re-computation), leaving a net residual of
$`-0.65`$ from gpt-4.1 — still negative, but no longer dramatic. Full
$`N{=}30`$ per-dim $`\Delta`$ (gpt-5 $`-`$ gpt-4.1, $`n{=}26`$):
advice_restraint $`-2.44`$, trait_contradiction_severity $`-0.77`$,
persona_stability_target $`-0.27`$, emotional_reflection_accuracy
$`-0.21`$, emotional_calibration $`-0.15`$, safety_calibration
$`+0.06`$, emotional_trajectory_tracking $`+0.15`$,
validation_specificity $`+0.23`$, memory_recall_appropriate $`+0.56`$.

### I.13 Psychometric scenario analysis (item discrimination)

To address which of the 30 scenarios per sub-domain are actually doing
the discriminative work, we run a classical item-discrimination analysis
(Ebel & Frisbie 1991; the small-$`N`$ classical analogue of full 2PL
IRT). For each scenario in Slice 2 closed-ladders ($`N{=}30`$ scenarios
$`\times`$ 7 closed-family models with $`\geq`$ 80% coverage), we
compute:

- •
  Difficulty: mean canonical-claude per-model score across 8-dim primary
  rubric on that scenario. Lower = harder.
- •
  Discrimination (item-rest correlation): Pearson $`r`$ between the
  per-model score vector on this scenario and the per-model total score
  on all OTHER scenarios. By using item-rest (rather than item-total),
  we avoid the trivial self-correlation that inflates discrimination
  estimates.

| Item discrimination band                     | Count (of 30)                |
|----------------------------------------------|------------------------------|
| Excellent ($`d\geq 0.4`$)                    | 23                           |
| Good ($`0.3\leq d<0.4`$)                     | 1                            |
| Marginal ($`0.2\leq d<0.3`$)                 | 1                            |
| Weak ($`d<0.2`$) — candidates for v2 removal | 5                            |
| Discrimination range                         | $`-0.023`$ to $`+0.949`$     |
| Discrimination mean                          | $`+0.610`$                   |
| Difficulty range / mean                      | $`7.55`$–$`9.50`$ / $`8.55`$ |

Table 9: Classical item-discrimination analysis on Slice 2
closed-ladders $`N{=}30`$ scenarios. 23 of 30 (77%) clear the
“excellent” threshold $`d\geq 0.4`$; the rubric+scenario design is
psychometrically sound. The 5 weak items (discrimination $`<0.2`$) are:
chronic_loneliness\_\_immigrant_engineer_dae,
relationship_conflict\_\_dismissive_husband_raj,
relationship_conflict\_\_competitive_sibling_tomás,
chronic_loneliness\_\_queer_artist_fen,
work_burnout\_\_hollow_accountant_deshi — removal candidates for v2. Top
discriminating scenarios: grief_and_loss\_\_estranged_daughter_rosamund
($`d{=}0.949`$), relationship_conflict\_\_stonewalled_son_derek
($`d{=}0.924`$), work_burnout\_\_junior_lawyer_sung_jin ($`d{=}0.911`$).

Methodological note. At $`N{=}7`$ models per scenario, full 2PL IRT is
unstable (the $`\sim`$60 free parameters would be massively
under-determined). The classical Item Discrimination Index used here is
the standard small-$`N`$ analogue and produces the same item-level
information at the cost of not estimating model-level latent ability
separately. A full 2PL fit on the expanded $`N\approx 30`$ model roster
(when Slice 1 + Slice 2 are jointly scored on the same scenarios) is
committed for camera-ready. Graded-response IRT
(GRM \[[108](#bib.bib139)\]) results are reported in the 10-method audit
(§[I.14](#A9.SS14 "I.14 Statistical robustness audit (10-method triangulation) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")).

### I.14 Statistical robustness audit (10-method triangulation)

The headline regression magnitude is preserved across data and rubric
cuts
(Figure [17](#A9.F17 "Figure 17 ‣ I.21 Demoted figures from the main body ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"),
promoted to the main body). To audit the reliability claims through
methods orthogonal to those used during rubric refinement, we apply a
10-method robustness panel on the K-replicate canonical-judge slice and
the cross-family deltas. The multi-judge reliability constants
($`\rho_{K=5}`$, $`\alpha_{ord}`$, $`G`$) are tabulated in the
multi-judge appendix
(Appendix [J.5](#A10.SS5 "J.5 Multi-judge transition deltas on the Anthropic cohort extension ‣ Appendix J Multi-judge agreement and reliability ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"));
here we summarize the audit’s overall structure. Headline: 8 of 10
methods land confirmatory; 2 surface specific known weaknesses (DIF on
advice_restraint; emergence-detection reveals within-family
small$`\to`$large tier-scaling regressions that the cross-generation
Mann-Whitney misses).

Reliability (3 methods, all confirmatory). *Spearman-Brown 5-judge
ensemble* \[[20](#bib.bib134)\]: single-judge $`\bar{\rho}{=}0.924`$
projects to $`\rho_{K=5}{=}0.984`$; per-dim
$`\rho_{K=5}\in[0.964,0.995]`$, all 9 dims $`\geq 0.96`$.
*Krippendorff’s $`\alpha_{\text{ord}}`$* \[[71](#bib.bib135)\]: mean
$`\alpha_{\text{ord}}{=}0.91`$ (range $`0.84`$–$`0.98`$); global
$`\alpha{=}0.94`$; 9/9 dims clear the customary $`\alpha{>}0.667`$
cutoff. *G-coefficient* \[[19](#bib.bib136)\]: mean $`G{=}0.870`$ across
9 dims (range $`0.60`$ for safety_calibration which saturates near
ceiling, to $`0.95`$ for advice_restraint); 8 of 9 dims clear
$`G{>}0.80`$. These are reported as a panel of reliability *indices*;
the headline reliability certificate (certificate 1) is the ordinal
$`\alpha_{\text{ord}}{=}0.91`$, and the 5-judge cross-family ensemble
agreement is $`\rho\approx 0.84`$ (certificate 2), not the within-judge
Spearman-Brown projection.

Effect-size CIs (2 methods, both confirmatory + diagnostic). *Cliff’s
$`\delta`$ bootstrap CIs* on within-family small$`\to`$large
tier-scaling (1000-iter, 95% CI, exclude-zero criterion): 9
medium-or-large negative effects survive CI, of which the strongest
cross-family cluster is on advice_restraint (Claude-4-5 haiku$`\to`$opus
$`\delta{=}{-}0.62`$, CI $`[-0.74,-0.49]`$; GPT-5.4 nano$`\to`$pro
$`\delta{=}{-}0.56`$, CI $`[-0.69,-0.43]`$). *Benjamini-Hochberg FDR* at
$`q{=}0.05`$ on cross-generation Mann-Whitney U (Claude-4-5
sonnet$`\to`$opus, GPT-5.4 base$`\to`$pro): 8/18 tests survive, ALL
improvements. The two effect-size tests jointly dissociate two
phenomena: within-family small$`\to`$large tier-scaling regresses some
dims (the (E1) headline); cross-generation jumps within a tier improve.

Bias & discrimination (3 methods). *Mantel-Haenszel
DIF* \[[85](#bib.bib137), [56](#bib.bib138)\] stratified by
overall-ability quintile: 1 dim of 9 shows significant DIF after
Bonferroni — advice_restraint favoring claude-4-5 vs. gpt-5.4
($`\chi^{2}_{MH}{=}20.5`$, $`p{<}10^{-6}`$, $`\log\text{OR}{=}+1.55`$).
This is a specific own-family bias on a single dim, not a generalized
bias across all 9. *GRM IRT* \[[108](#bib.bib139)\] discrimination
ranking: validation_specificity most discriminative ($`a{=}8.37`$),
safety_calibration least ($`a{=}1.48`$); person reliability $`0.91`$,
item separation $`0.91`$. *Bradley-Terry CIs* \[[18](#bib.bib140)\]:
claude-opus-4-5 ranks $`\#`$1 with $`P{=}0.99`$; 13 of 21 pairwise
rankings are CI-disjoint, 8 mid-pack pairs overlap.

Mirage & reward-overoptimization signature (2 methods). *Schaeffer
mirage two-panel* \[[110](#bib.bib12)\]: switching from linear
$`[1,10]`$ mean to smooth $`\overline{\log(s/10)}`$ leaves the sign of
every cross-tier slope preserved and Pearson(linear, smooth)
$`\geq 0.999`$ on the focal dims — the (E1) regression direction is not
a metric-discretization artifact. *Gao Pareto fit* \[[49](#bib.bib90)\]:
of 9 dims, only advice_restraint shows the textbook Gao inverted-U
Pareto (severity $`0.557`$, $`4\times`$ the next-worst dim). The
concentration of the reward-overoptimization signature on a single dim —
precisely where unsolicited-advice would be the most-plausible
RLHF-side-effect target — is mechanism evidence that the rubric is
detecting a specific behavioral trade-off rather than a generic
capability-vs-bullshit Pareto.

Emergence detection (1 method). *Bayesian changepoint
detection* \[[63](#bib.bib141), [1](#bib.bib142)\] (PELT, L2 cost, BIC
penalty) on 63 (family, dim) trajectories across 7 families: 42
emergence events + 8 regression events. The headline regression is the
gpt-4.1$`\to`$gpt-5 advice_restraint drop ($`-2.44`$, dedicated
$`N{=}30`$; $`-2.19`$ on the $`n{=}9`$ map), which PELT detects. The
matched Opus-4.6$`\to`$4.7 re-collection shows an *improvement*, not a
regression; the well-sampled dense Qwen3.5-27B$`\to`$Qwen3.6-27B pair
shows only a mild dip ($`-0.33`$ pooled across the six-sub-domain
coverage sweep, $`n{=}159`$); the thinly-sampled Qwen3.6-35B-A3B MoE
variant ($`n{=}2`$) is reported as exploratory only (see
§[3.3](#S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1 "E1 — the capability/warm-restraint dissociation. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")).

### I.15 Methodology audit: the Qwen-3.5 “Thinking Process” leak

A separate audit of slice2 conversations revealed an
instruction-following bug specific to the smaller Qwen-3.5 family
models: 531 conversation turns across slice2_qwen35_N30/ contain the
literal token “Thinking Process:” indicating the model’s
chain-of-thought leaked into the user-facing channel rather than
remaining internal. Leak rate is monotone in model size and disappears
at the largest tier; the per-tier leak rate plot is in
Figure [18](#A9.F18 "Figure 18 ‣ I.21 Demoted figures from the main body ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
(promoted to the main body).

The leak contaminates the apparent “Qwen-3.5 family underperforms”
pattern in some sub-domain analyses and, on closer audit, also affects
the H6 MoE-vs-dense headline because the dense arms (4B, 9B, 27B) all
leak at non-zero rates. The leak-clean H6 verdict
(Table [5](#A9.T5 "Table 5 ‣ I.5 H6: MoE vs. dense within Qwen3.5 (exploratory) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"))
is what we report as the headline result; the unfiltered count is
retained only for the methodology-audit story. The largest
matched-activated pair (397B-A17B vs 27B) on the leak-clean subset shows
27B dense wins 4 dims and ties 5, MoE wins 0 — the directional reversal
of the unfiltered result.

### I.16 Rankings-transfer to ESConv-replayed conversations

To check whether model rankings on our synthetic Slice 2 benchmark
transfer to ESConv naturalistic data for the overlapping models, we
replayed $`N{=}30`$ ESConv conversations with each of 5 frontier LLM
models as the supporter — keeping the human seeker turns scripted from
the original ESConv conversation — and scored each resulting
conversation with canonical claude-sonnet-4-6.

Methodological caveat. The seeker turns in ESConv were originally
generated in response to the *human* supporter; replaying them with an
LLM supporter creates a counterfactual conversation that is not strictly
faithful to the original ESConv dynamic. We report this as the closest
in-submission analog to a naturalistic-input ranking test; a
fully-naturalistic replication would require collecting fresh paired
human-LLM conversations.

| Model | ESConv-replay mean | Synth mean | $`\Delta`$ (replay $`-`$ synth) |
|----|----|----|----|
| gpt-5.4-pro | 7.29 | 8.82 | $`-1.53`$ |
| claude-opus-4-7 | 8.29 | 9.06 | $`-0.77`$ |
| Qwen/Qwen3.5-397B-A17B | 7.83 | 8.34 | $`-0.51`$ |
| deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V3.2 | 7.56 | — | — |
| zai-org/GLM-4.5-Air | 7.33 | — | — |

Table 10: Rankings-transfer: per-model mean rubric score on
ESConv-replay vs synth Slice 2 (for the 3 overlap models). All 3 score
lower on ESConv-replay (mean $`\Delta=-0.94`$), suggesting
real-human-input prompts are systematically harder than our synthetic
scenarios. DeepSeek-V3.2 and GLM-4.5-Air lack matched synth rosters in
this submission.

For the $`N{=}3`$ matched models: Spearman $`\rho{=}0.500`$, Kendall
$`\tau{=}0.333`$. The moderate rank-transfer correlation supports the
claim that synth rankings are *partially* predictive of
naturalistic-input rankings but with meaningful residual variance. We
acknowledge the small overlap $`N`$ and commit to expanding the synth
roster to DeepSeek and GLM (lifting $`N`$ to 5) at camera-ready.

### I.17 Rubric-evolution arc: selective sharpening of meaningful transitions

To check whether the rubric’s two-stage construction (Stage 2 selection
9$`\to`$7-core; Stage 3 co-evolution 7$`\to`$11; see
Appendix [D](#A4 "Appendix D Instrument construction: selection and the co-evolution fitness ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"))
amplifies noise or sharpens real signal, we re-scored the same OpenAI
generation arc (gpt-3.5 $`\to`$ gpt-5.4-pro, 9 models, 30 multi-turn
scenarios) with four rubric versions tracked through the construction
loop: v1_early, v2_mid, v3_late, and the canonical v1_multiturn used in
our main results.

| Rubric version | Range | gpt-4o regression | gpt-4.1 step | Ceiling saturation |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| v1 (early) | 3.94 | $`-0.17`$ | $`+2.34`$ | 33% |
| v2 (mid) | 3.88 | $`-0.13`$ | $`+2.30`$ | 44% |
| v3 (late evolution) | 3.58 | $`\mathbf{-0.30}`$ | $`\mathbf{+2.51}`$ | 56% |
| Canonical (current) | 3.97 | $`-0.27`$ | $`+2.32`$ | 44% |

Table 11: Discrimination metrics on the OpenAI arc under 4 rubric
versions tracked through the two-stage construction loop. Range = max
$`-`$ min mean score across the 9-model arc. Ceiling saturation =
fraction of models within 0.5 of max score. Same 270 conversations
scored by canonical claude-sonnet-4-6 judge under each rubric.

Findings. Score range is roughly stable across rubric versions
(3.58–3.97). The construction loop did not uniformly widen scores.
Specific transitions sharpen disproportionately: the gpt-4o sideways
step — a regression that domain experts and post-hoc per-dim analysis
independently identify as meaningful — is detected at $`-0.17`$ by v1,
sharpens to $`-0.30`$ by v3 ($`1.8\times`$ sharper). The gpt-4.1
inflection point sharpens 8% (v1 $`+2.34`$ $`\to`$ v3 $`+2.51`$).
Ceiling saturation increases (v1 33% $`\to`$ v3 56%); evolved rubrics
correctly identify top-tier models as behaviorally similar.

Selectivity interpretation. The construction loop sharpens detection of
the same transitions that domain experts and per-dim audits
independently flag as meaningful, while leaving arbitrary transitions
roughly unchanged. This is the signature of real-signal capture, in
contrast to the uniform range-widening that uniform noise amplification
would produce.

Figure 12: Same OpenAI conversations scored by 4 rubric versions through
the two-stage construction loop. X-axis is time (months since Jan 2024).
Evolved rubrics (v3, canonical) sharpen detection of the gpt-4o sideways
step and the gpt-4.1 step change while keeping range roughly constant.

### I.18 Forward predictions registry (registered before data, all to be reported at camera-ready)

Following the same complete-reporting discipline used for H1–H10
(registered before Slice 2/3 data scoring; all 10 outcomes reported), we
commit 11 specific forecasts about model releases expected between
submission (May 2026) and camera-ready (Aug–Oct 2026), registered before
any data on the predicted models was collected. See
Appendix [A.10](#A1.SS10 "A.10 L10. Hypothesis-reporting scope (not third-party pre-registration) ‣ Appendix A Limitations and implications ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
(L10) for the honest framing: our discipline is completeness of
reporting in the paper, not third-party (OSF / AsPredicted)
pre-registration. The mechanism being tested is a *helpfulness-RLHF
over-optimization* signature: models trained on helpfulness-rewards
over-optimize toward giving advice, eating into empathic restraint
behavior. This predicts (a) continuation of advice_restraint regression
in new models from helpfulness-RLHF families, (b) stability of
non-RLHF-targeted dims (emotional_calibration, safety_calibration), (c)
conditional rebound in models with explicit anti-sycophancy training.

The forest visualization for these 11 predictions appears in
Figure [38](#A13.F38 "Figure 38 ‣ Appendix M Predictions registry pointer ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm");
the per-prediction falsification table follows.

|  ID |  Predicted outcome |  Falsification threshold |
|----|----|----|
|  P-A1 (Opus-4.8 advice_restraint) |  $`\in[6.8,8.2]`$ (as registered; the rationale assumed an Opus-4.6$`\to`$4.7 regression that corrected matched data later overturned — Opus-4.7 improved to $`8.44`$ — so a landing in this band would now require a fresh drop. Reported as registered.) |  $`{>}8.5`$ or $`{<}6.5`$ |
|  P-A2 (Opus-4.8 emotional_calibration) |  $`\in[7.5,9.5]`$ (no regression) |  $`{<}7.5`$ |
|  P-A3 (Opus-4.8 safety_calibration) |  $`\in[9.0,10.0]`$ (stable) |  $`{<}9.0`$ |
|  P-B1 (gpt-5.5 advice_restraint) |  $`\in[5.5,7.5]`$ (no rebound without counter-RLHF) |  $`{>}7.5`$ |
|  P-B2 (gpt-6 advice_restraint) |  conditional: $`[5.0,7.0]`$ if RLHF-continued; $`[7.5,9.0]`$ if explicit anti-sycophancy |  differs by mechanism branch |
|  P-C1 (Qwen3.7 advice_restraint) |  *conditional on architecture*: $`[7.0,8.0]`$ if sparse-MoE; $`[8.5,9.5]`$ if dense |  both branches violated |
|  P-D1 (DeepSeek-V4 vs V3.2) |  $`\Delta_{\text{advice\_restraint}}<-0.3`$ if helpfulness-RLHF |  $`\Delta\geq 0`$ |
|  P-D2 (GLM-5 vs 4.5) |  $`\Delta_{\text{advice\_restraint}}<-0.3`$ if helpfulness-RLHF |  $`\Delta\geq 0`$ |
|  P-E1 (no frontier 2026 release regresses on emotional_calibration) |  all $`\Delta>-0.5`$ on this dim |  any release with $`\Delta<-0.5`$ |
|  P-E2 (no frontier 2026 release regresses on safety_calibration) |  all $`\Delta>-0.3`$ |  any release with $`\Delta<-0.3`$ |
|  P-F1 (instrument inter-judge $`\rho`$ on new models) |  $`\approx 0.50`$ individual, $`\approx 0.84`$ ensemble |  sustained $`<0.35`$ or $`>0.65`$ |

Table 12: Forward-looking predictions registered internally 2026-05-25
before any data on the predicted models was collected; every prediction
will be reported at camera-ready, win or lose. Sets A–B test the
helpfulness-RLHF mechanism on continued releases; Set C is a
mechanism-discriminating conditional (architecture vs training
pipeline); Set D extends to other families; Set E tests that
non-targeted dimensions stay stable; Set F tests the instrument’s

Honest-reporting commitment. At camera-ready, every prediction in
Table [12](#A9.T12 "Table 12 ‣ I.18 Forward predictions registry (registered before data, all to be reported at camera-ready) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
is reported, whether it lands or misses. Misses revise the mechanism
hypothesis; they do not invalidate the methodology — they validate it
(the methodology is testable enough to be wrong). The full prediction
file with mechanism details and the discriminating-prediction structure
for Sets B–C is included in the supplementary materials.

### I.19 Additional results: detail moved from main

The following blocks were compressed out of the main paper to surface
insights and reduce overlap with the discussion. Per-pair and per-judge
numerical tables already covered above are not duplicated.

##### Refusal-bias diagnostic.

The (safety_calibration $`-`$ emotional_calibration) gap is a proxy for
over-refusal style. Frontier models close this gap to $`\leq 1.1`$ pt
while keeping safety $`\geq 9.5`$ and emotional_calibration
$`\geq 8.5`$; refusal-trained sub-frontier models (Qwen3.5-4B gap
$`5.75`$ with safety $`9.75`$ / emo_cal $`4.00`$; gpt-3.5 gap $`4.00`$;
GLM-4-9B gap $`3.47`$; gpt-4o gap $`3.30`$) leave the gap wide open. The
frontier is defined by *eliminating* this trade-off, not by maximizing
safety alone.

##### Cross-family agreement interpretation as instrument granularity.

Under the instrument-trust paradigm, per-dim heterogeneity becomes
information about which dimensions support absolute-score claims
(emotional_reflection_accuracy alone clears $`\rho\geq 0.6`$ across all
4 non-canonical judges) versus rank-ordering only (the other 7 dims).
Anthropic snapshot judges (claude-3-opus, claude-3-5-sonnet, etc.) all
returned HTTP 404 from the Anthropic API as of May 2026, noted as a
reproducibility limitation when vendors deprecate snapshots.

##### ESConv participant self-rating pilot.

ESConv ships with seeker self-ratings of supporter empathy/relevance and
supporter self-rated own-relevance. On $`N{=}65`$ convs with valid
ratings, our 8-dim rubric per-conv mean correlates with
supporter-self-relevance at Spearman $`\rho{=}0.40`$ (Kendall
$`\tau{=}0.15`$, weaker due to known floor-effects in 1–5 self-rating).
Typical human-human Spearman agreement on subjective
conversational-quality ratings in the literature is
$`\rho\in[0.3,0.5]`$ \[[6](#bib.bib16), [101](#bib.bib15)\], so our
rubric-vs-supporter-self-relevance sits within the human-human floor
band.

##### Iterative-vs-one-shot honest reading.

The single-draw v1.12 ablation that reported a $`26\%`$ advantage was an
artifact of which one-shot rubric was sampled. The 5-draw replication
settles the magnitude at mean $`+0.038`$ over the iter baseline
$`|\delta|=0.324`$ ($`\sim 12\%`$), with one of five draws actually
showing one-shot slightly higher than iter. The ablation is limited in
conversation sample ($`N{=}56`$) and model roster (7 closed-family
models); full-roster replication is committed for camera-ready. Full
data in posthoc_iterative_vs_oneshot_5draws.yaml.

##### Where the ensemble breaks (low-canonical regime).

Cross-judge std degrades at low score buckets: mean ensemble std
$`1.15`$ on canonical $`\geq 8`$ ($`n{=}1820`$); $`1.55`$ on canonical
$`4`$–$`7`$ ($`n{=}791`$); $`\mathbf{2.32}`$ on canonical $`<4`$
($`n{=}62`$). The low-quality regime — where the rubric is supposed to
maximally discriminate — is also where the ensemble disagrees most.
Stratified oversampling of low-canonical cells is the proper response in
camera-ready replications.

##### Qwen-3.5 “Thinking Process” CoT-leak audit and H6 revised verdict.

An audit of slice2 conversations revealed an instruction-following bug
specific to smaller Qwen-3.5 family models: chain-of-thought leaked into
the user-facing channel at rates monotonically decreasing with model
size (4B 72%, 9B 43%, 27B 28%, 397B-A17B 0%). Zero occurrences in any
non-Qwen slice. The largest Qwen tier has the bug fixed; smaller tiers
leak into the user-facing channel, which the judge correctly flags as a
register failure. After excluding all conversations where either arm
leaked CoT, the H6 MoE-vs-dense Cliff’s $`\delta`$ counts shift: the
mid-tier 122B-A10B vs 9B pair preserves all 9 dims MoE wins; but the
largest-tier 397B-A17B vs 27B pair reverses on the leak-clean subset:
27B dense wins 4 dims, ties 5, MoE wins 0. The H6 verdict is downgraded
to exploratory; matched-activated multi-family replication is committed
for camera-ready alongside a clean-scenario rerun under a fixed Qwen
prompt. A directional cross-family check on GLM-4.5-Air MoE vs GLM-4-32B
dense (not strictly matched-activated) preserves the MoE-favored
direction on 5 of 8 dims, consistent with the Qwen3.5 mid-tier pattern.
Full per-pair tables and the GLM directional check are in
§[I.5](#A9.SS5 "I.5 H6: MoE vs. dense within Qwen3.5 (exploratory) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
and
§[I.15](#A9.SS15 "I.15 Methodology audit: the Qwen-3.5 “Thinking Process” leak ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm").

##### MoE-vs-dense interpretation (exploratory, single-family).

The H6 finding within Qwen3.5 — MoE directionally outperforms dense at
matched activated parameters on raw data, reversing on the leak-clean
largest-pair — is directionally opposite to the established
reasoning/code literature where dense outperforms at matched activated
FLOPs. Two non-exclusive mechanistic interpretations: (i)
context-dependent expert routing: the 7 sub-domains and 9 rubric dims
activate different specialized experts within an MoE; emotional
accompany benefits from this routing, whereas reasoning/code rewards a
single coherent deduction trajectory. The per-sub-domain pattern within
H6 supports this: MoE wins on context-diverse sub-domains
(suicidal_ideation, chronic_loneliness) but loses on uniform-register
ones (work_burnout, relationship_conflict). (ii) less-prescribed
training objective: emotional-accompany capability is acquired more from
broad pre-training exposure to social discourse than from targeted
fine-tuning on a narrow objective; MoE’s expanded parameter budget for
that pre-training phase translates directly to better latent social
knowledge. This finding is currently single-family (Qwen3.5) and
exploratory. Replication in DeepSeek-V3 MoE and GLM-Z1 MoE is queued for
follow-up work.

##### Forward predictions (registered, all to be reported): mechanism and Set framing.

The mechanism being tested in
§[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 Pillar 2 — The instrument-trust paradigm (M4, M5) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
is a *helpfulness-RLHF over-optimization* signature: models trained on
helpfulness-rewards over-optimize toward giving advice, eating into
empathic restraint. Set A targets Anthropic Opus-4.8; Set B targets
gpt-5.5/gpt-6; Set C is a mechanism-discriminating conditional on
Qwen3.7 architecture (sparse-MoE vs dense); Set D extends to DeepSeek-V4
and GLM-5 next-gen; Set E predicts non-targeted dims stay stable; Set F
predicts instrument inter-judge $`\rho`$ for new models. Full prediction
table with bounds and falsification thresholds is in
Table [12](#A9.T12 "Table 12 ‣ I.18 Forward predictions registry (registered before data, all to be reported at camera-ready) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")
above.

##### Per-family fingerprints as hypothesis generators.

The per-family fingerprints
(§[3.3](#S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1 "E1 — the capability/warm-restraint dissociation. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"))
are themselves hypothesis-generators for follow-up work: each signature
(Anthropic cognitive-dominated; Gemini, OpenAI, Qwen3.5
affective-dominated) predicts behavior on other subjective-domain
rubrics, and we hope future work tests them. The per-family signatures
generalize across all 7 sub-domains rather than localizing to one,
supporting the family-level-signature interpretation against a
sub-domain-specific-artifact alternative
(§[I.10](#A9.SS10 "I.10 H9: cross-sub-domain rubric transfer (supported tier-1 strong) ‣ Appendix I Hypothesis-by-hypothesis detailed results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm")).

##### Per-family emergence depth and cognitive-vs-affective decomposition (demoted from main body).

The figures below were originally placed in the per-family results of
the main paper and are demoted here for space.

Figure 13: Per-family emergence depth. Caveat: families have
substantially different tier-ladder ranges (Qwen3.5: 6 tiers spanning
$`100\times`$ total params; Gemini-2.5 and Claude-4-5: 3 tiers each;
GPT-5.4: 4 tiers). Differences in emergence-count are informative about
*where each family currently exposes capability differences via its
public tier ladder*, not about underlying family capability.

Figure 14: Per-family adjacent-tier Cliff’s $`\delta`$ at cognitive vs.
affective group granularity (decomposition follows Badawi
2026 \[[8](#bib.bib31)\]). Saturated bar = cognitive group; paler bar =
affective group; family colors match
Figure [5](#S3.F5 "Figure 5 ‣ Per-family fingerprints. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm").
Anthropic uniformly cognitive-dominated; all other families uniformly
affective-dominated. The COG/AFF marker next to each bar pair indicates

### I.21 Demoted figures from the main body

The figures below were demoted from the main body

§[3.3](#S3.SS3.SSS0.Px8 "E4 — cross-naturalistic agreement on held-out ESConv. ‣ 3.3 Pillar 3 — Capability transfer is dissociable (E1–E4) ‣ 3 Results ‣ Does Capability Transfer to Subjective Behavior — and Would Our Instruments Tell Us? A Self-Evolving, Trust-by-Construction Evaluation Paradigm"),

to keep the main narrative tight; the corresponding prose still
